I haven't really stuck around to watch the other guys at the shop use one since this all got so bad, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.1, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I've gotten to the point where I'll do almost anything to avoid using the straight razor at work. I'm a barber, and I'll switch clients with someone else, or I'll say the blade is missing, or I'll just hide it in the drawer. And if I absolutely can't get out of it, I'm just shaking through the whole shave. It's gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience excessive fear or anxiety in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... outside of the shop, I'm generally fine. I haven't noticed being overly anxious about anything else. It's just, uh, it's really just dealing with that razor. If I'm not at work having to do a shave, I don't have that problem at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of accidentally cutting someone with the razor when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool that I trained with, and I can use clippers all day long without a second thought. But... when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, it really feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or even cut myself really badly.\n\nDoctor: Is it the razor itself that scares you, or is it more the thought of what could happen if you were to make a mistake with it?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a little bit of both. I mean, the thought of making a mistake and really hurting someone is definitely what's in my head. But at this point... honestly, just looking at the razor itself makes me panic. Just seeing the blade sitting there on the counter is enough to make me start shaking, even before I pick it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious if you were to handle the straight razor in a non-work setting, like at home?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I don't keep one at home, so I haven't really tested it out. But given how I react just seeing it on my station at work, I'd probably still feel pretty anxious if I had to hold one anywhere else. The blade itself just really gets to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel less anxious about using the straight razor if you were alone in the shop, with no clients present?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make much of a difference, honestly. Even if the shop was completely empty... just the thought of holding it makes my chest tight. It's not just about the clients anymore. Like I said, just seeing the blade sitting there sets me off, so being alone probably wouldn't stop the shaking or the panic.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you had to use a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving a client, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually tried to shave myself with one since all this started, so I don't know for absolute sure. I haven't tried having someone stand by or help me with it since the fear got this bad. But... I mean, I doubt it would help much. \n\nIt's the razor itself that causes the panic. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be just as anxious, even if someone I trusted was watching or trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about using the straight razor would be less intense if you were alone with the razor, but not at work?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. Like I said, just seeing the blade sets me off. It wouldn't really matter if I was at home, in my car, or at the shop. The razor is the problem, not where I am when I'm looking at it. I think I'd still have the exact same reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to see someone else holding or using it, even if you weren't the one using it?\n\nPatient: I haven't really stuck around to watch the other guys at the shop use one since this all got so bad, so I don't know for absolute sure. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.1, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I've gotten to the point where I'll do almost anything to avoid using the straight razor at work. I'm a barber, and I'll switch clients with someone else, or I'll say the blade is missing, or I'll just hide it in the drawer. And if I absolutely can't get out of it, I'm just shaking through the whole shave. It's gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience excessive fear or anxiety in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... outside of the shop, I'm generally fine. I haven't noticed being overly anxious about anything else. It's just, uh, it's really just dealing with that razor. If I'm not at work having to do a shave, I don't have that problem at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of accidentally cutting someone with the razor when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool that I trained with, and I can use clippers all day long without a second thought. But... when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, it really feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or even cut myself really badly.\n\nDoctor: Is it the razor itself that scares you, or is it more the thought of what could happen if you were to make a mistake with it?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a little bit of both. I mean, the thought of making a mistake and really hurting someone is definitely what's in my head. But at this point... honestly, just looking at the razor itself makes me panic. Just seeing the blade sitting there on the counter is enough to make me start shaking, even before I pick it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious if you were to handle the straight razor in a non-work setting, like at home?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I don't keep one at home, so I haven't really tested it out. But given how I react just seeing it on my station at work, I'd probably still feel pretty anxious if I had to hold one anywhere else. The blade itself just really gets to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel less anxious about using the straight razor if you were alone in the shop, with no clients present?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make much of a difference, honestly. Even if the shop was completely empty... just the thought of holding it makes my chest tight. It's not just about the clients anymore. Like I said, just seeing the blade sitting there sets me off, so being alone probably wouldn't stop the shaking or the panic.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you had to use a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving a client, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually tried to shave myself with one since all this started, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... honestly, I think it would be just as bad. \n\nLike I was saying, just looking at the razor on the counter makes me shake now. So the idea of holding that same blade up to my own face or neck... yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be terrified. Just the thought of it makes my stomach drop.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using the straight razor decrease if you were being supervised or assisted by someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't tried having someone stand by or help me with it since the fear got this bad. But... I mean, I doubt it would help much. \n\nIt's the razor itself that causes the panic. Having a friend or my boss right there wouldn't change the fact that I still have to hold the blade. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be just as anxious, even if someone I trusted was watching or trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about using the straight razor would be less intense if you were alone with the razor, but not at work?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. Like I said, just seeing the blade sets me off. It wouldn't really matter if I was at home, in my car, or at the shop. The razor is the problem, not where I am when I'm looking at it. I think I'd still have the exact same reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to see someone else holding or using it, even if you weren't the one using it?\n\nPatient: I haven't really stuck around to watch the other guys at the shop use one since this all got so bad, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, it really feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or even cut myself really badly.\n\nDoctor: Is it the razor itself that scares you, or is it more the thought of what could happen if you were to make a mistake with it?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a little bit of both. I mean, the thought of making a mistake and really hurting someone is definitely what's in my head. But at this point... honestly, just looking at the razor itself makes me panic. Just seeing the blade sitting there on the counter is enough to make me start shaking, even before I pick it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious if you were to handle the straight razor in a non-work setting, like at home?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I don't keep one at home, so I haven't really tested it out. But given how I react just seeing it on my station at work, I'd probably still feel pretty anxious if I had to hold one anywhere else. The blade itself just really gets to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel less anxious about using the straight razor if you were alone in the shop, with no clients present?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make much of a difference, honestly. Even if the shop was completely empty... just the thought of holding it makes my chest tight. It's not just about the clients anymore. Like I said, just seeing the blade sitting there sets me off, so being alone probably wouldn't stop the shaking or the panic.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you had to use a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving a client, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually tried to shave myself with one since all this started, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... honestly, I think it would be just as bad. \n\nLike I was saying, just looking at the razor on the counter makes me shake now. So the idea of holding that same blade up to my own face or neck... yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be terrified. Just the thought of it makes my stomach drop.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using the straight razor decrease if you were being supervised or assisted by someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't tried having someone stand by or help me with it since the fear got this bad. But... I mean, I doubt it would help much. \n\nIt's the razor itself that causes the panic. Having a friend or my boss right there wouldn't change the fact that I still have to hold the blade. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be just as anxious, even if someone I trusted was watching or trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about using the straight razor would be less intense if you were alone with the razor, but not at work?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. Like I said, just seeing the blade sets me off. It wouldn't really matter if I was at home, in my car, or at the shop. The razor is the problem, not where I am when I'm looking at it. I think I'd still have the exact same reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to see someone else holding or using it, even if you weren't the one using it?\n\nPatient: I haven't really stuck around to watch the other guys at the shop use one since this all got so bad, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... I mean, given how much just seeing a razor sitting on the counter bothers me, I imagine watching someone else hold it or use it would still make me really anxious. \n\nI don't know if it would be the exact same level of panic as having it in my own hand, but... yeah, I definitely wouldn't be comfortable anywhere near it, even if someone else was doing the shaving.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor decrease if you were to use it for a task that is not related to your work as a barber?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I haven't tried using it for anything else, but... the fear is really about the razor itself, not what I'm doing with it. \n\nEven if I was just using it to, I don't know, cut a piece of string or something at home, I'm pretty sure I'd still have the exact same panic. Just holding the blade, or even looking at it, is what causes the problem. So no, I don't think the task would make a difference.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be present if you were to encounter it in a completely unrelated context, such as a museum or an antique shop?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually run into one in a place like that, so I don't know for a fact. But... honestly, I'm pretty sure the anxiety would still be there. \n\nLike I was saying before, just the sight of the blade is what gets to me. So if I was walking through an antique store and just suddenly saw one sitting there on a shelf... yeah, I think my heart would still start racing. The context doesn't really seem to matter, it's just the razor itself.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you saw a straight razor being used for shaving in a movie or on television?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't actually seen one on TV or in a movie lately... at least, not since this whole thing got so bad. \n\nSo I don't know for a fact how I'd react to just seeing it on a screen. I might be okay since it's not real life, or it might still bother me, but I just haven't had that happen recently so I can't really say.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I'm a barber, and I'll switch clients with someone else, or I'll say the blade is missing, or I'll just hide it in the drawer. And if I absolutely can't get out of it, I'm just shaking through the whole shave. It's gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience excessive fear or anxiety in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... outside of the shop, I'm generally fine. I haven't noticed being overly anxious about anything else. It's just, uh, it's really just dealing with that razor. If I'm not at work having to do a shave, I don't have that problem at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of accidentally cutting someone with the razor when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool that I trained with, and I can use clippers all day long without a second thought. But... when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, it really feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or even cut myself really badly.\n\nDoctor: Is it the razor itself that scares you, or is it more the thought of what could happen if you were to make a mistake with it?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a little bit of both. I mean, the thought of making a mistake and really hurting someone is definitely what's in my head. But at this point... honestly, just looking at the razor itself makes me panic. Just seeing the blade sitting there on the counter is enough to make me start shaking, even before I pick it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious if you were to handle the straight razor in a non-work setting, like at home?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I don't keep one at home, so I haven't really tested it out. But given how I react just seeing it on my station at work, I'd probably still feel pretty anxious if I had to hold one anywhere else. The blade itself just really gets to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel less anxious about using the straight razor if you were alone in the shop, with no clients present?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make much of a difference, honestly. Even if the shop was completely empty... just the thought of holding it makes my chest tight. It's not just about the clients anymore. Like I said, just seeing the blade sitting there sets me off, so being alone probably wouldn't stop the shaking or the panic.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you had to use a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving a client, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually tried to shave myself with one since all this started, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... honestly, I think it would be just as bad. \n\nLike I was saying, just looking at the razor on the counter makes me shake now. So the idea of holding that same blade up to my own face or neck... yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be terrified. Just the thought of it makes my stomach drop.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using the straight razor decrease if you were being supervised or assisted by someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't tried having someone stand by or help me with it since the fear got this bad. But... I mean, I doubt it would help much. \n\nIt's the razor itself that causes the panic. Having a friend or my boss right there wouldn't change the fact that I still have to hold the blade. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be just as anxious, even if someone I trusted was watching or trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about using the straight razor would be less intense if you were alone with the razor, but not at work?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. Like I said, just seeing the blade sets me off. It wouldn't really matter if I was at home, in my car, or at the shop. The razor is the problem, not where I am when I'm looking at it. I think I'd still have the exact same reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to see someone else holding or using it, even if you weren't the one using it?\n\nPatient: I haven't really stuck around to watch the other guys at the shop use one since this all got so bad, so I don't know for absolute sure. I mean, I haven't tried using it for anything else, but... the fear is really about the razor itself, not what I'm doing with it. \n\nEven if I was just using it to, I don't know, cut a piece of string or something at home, I'm pretty sure I'd still have the exact same panic. Just holding the blade, or even looking at it, is what causes the problem. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I've gotten to the point where I'll do almost anything to avoid using the straight razor at work. I'm a barber, and I'll switch clients with someone else, or I'll say the blade is missing, or I'll just hide it in the drawer. And if I absolutely can't get out of it, I'm just shaking through the whole shave. It's gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience excessive fear or anxiety in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... outside of the shop, I'm generally fine. I haven't noticed being overly anxious about anything else. It's just, uh, it's really just dealing with that razor. If I'm not at work having to do a shave, I don't have that problem at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of accidentally cutting someone with the razor when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool that I trained with, and I can use clippers all day long without a second thought. But... when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, it really feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or even cut myself really badly.\n\nDoctor: Is it the razor itself that scares you, or is it more the thought of what could happen if you were to make a mistake with it?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a little bit of both. I mean, the thought of making a mistake and really hurting someone is definitely what's in my head. But at this point... honestly, just looking at the razor itself makes me panic. Just seeing the blade sitting there on the counter is enough to make me start shaking, even before I pick it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious if you were to handle the straight razor in a non-work setting, like at home?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I don't keep one at home, so I haven't really tested it out. But given how I react just seeing it on my station at work, I'd probably still feel pretty anxious if I had to hold one anywhere else. The blade itself just really gets to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel less anxious about using the straight razor if you were alone in the shop, with no clients present?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make much of a difference, honestly. Even if the shop was completely empty... just the thought of holding it makes my chest tight. It's not just about the clients anymore. Like I said, just seeing the blade sitting there sets me off, so being alone probably wouldn't stop the shaking or the panic.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you had to use a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving a client, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually tried to shave myself with one since all this started, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... honestly, I think it would be just as bad. \n\nLike I was saying, just looking at the razor on the counter makes me shake now. So the idea of holding that same blade up to my own face or neck... yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be terrified. Just the thought of it makes my stomach drop.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using the straight razor decrease if you were being supervised or assisted by someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't tried having someone stand by or help me with it since the fear got this bad. But... I mean, I doubt it would help much. \n\nIt's the razor itself that causes the panic. Having a friend or my boss right there wouldn't change the fact that I still have to hold the blade. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be just as anxious, even if someone I trusted was watching or trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about using the straight razor would be less intense if you were alone with the razor, but not at work?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. Like I said, just seeing the blade sets me off. It wouldn't really matter if I was at home, in my car, or at the shop. The razor is the problem, not where I am when I'm looking at it. I think I'd still have the exact same reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to see someone else holding or using it, even if you weren't the one using it?\n\nPatient: I haven't really stuck around to watch the other guys at the shop use one since this all got so bad, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... I mean, given how much just seeing a razor sitting on the counter bothers me, I imagine watching someone else hold it or use it would still make me really anxious. \n\nI don't know if it would be the exact same level of panic as having it in my own hand, but... yeah, I definitely wouldn't be comfortable anywhere near it, even if someone else was doing the shaving.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor decrease if you were to use it for a task that is not related to your work as a barber?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I haven't tried using it for anything else, but... the fear is really about the razor itself, not what I'm doing with it. \n\nEven if I was just using it to, I don't know, cut a piece of string or something at home, I'm pretty sure I'd still have the exact same panic. Just holding the blade, or even looking at it, is what causes the problem. So no, I don't think the task would make a difference.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be present if you were to encounter it in a completely unrelated context, such as a museum or an antique shop?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually run into one in a place like that, so I don't know for a fact. But... honestly, I'm pretty sure the anxiety would still be there. \n\nLike I was saying before, just the sight of the blade is what gets to me. So if I was walking through an antique store and just suddenly saw one sitting there on a shelf... yeah, I think my heart would still start racing. The context doesn't really seem to matter, it's just the razor itself.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you saw a straight razor being used for shaving in a movie or on television?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I'm a barber, and I'll switch clients with someone else, or I'll say the blade is missing, or I'll just hide it in the drawer. And if I absolutely can't get out of it, I'm just shaking through the whole shave. It's gotten really bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience excessive fear or anxiety in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... outside of the shop, I'm generally fine. I haven't noticed being overly anxious about anything else. It's just, uh, it's really just dealing with that razor. If I'm not at work having to do a shave, I don't have that problem at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of accidentally cutting someone with the razor when you're at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool that I trained with, and I can use clippers all day long without a second thought. But... when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, it really feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or even cut myself really badly.\n\nDoctor: Is it the razor itself that scares you, or is it more the thought of what could happen if you were to make a mistake with it?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a little bit of both. I mean, the thought of making a mistake and really hurting someone is definitely what's in my head. But at this point... honestly, just looking at the razor itself makes me panic. Just seeing the blade sitting there on the counter is enough to make me start shaking, even before I pick it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious if you were to handle the straight razor in a non-work setting, like at home?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I don't keep one at home, so I haven't really tested it out. But given how I react just seeing it on my station at work, I'd probably still feel pretty anxious if I had to hold one anywhere else. The blade itself just really gets to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel less anxious about using the straight razor if you were alone in the shop, with no clients present?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make much of a difference, honestly. Even if the shop was completely empty... just the thought of holding it makes my chest tight. It's not just about the clients anymore. Like I said, just seeing the blade sitting there sets me off, so being alone probably wouldn't stop the shaking or the panic.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you had to use a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving a client, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually tried to shave myself with one since all this started, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... honestly, I think it would be just as bad. \n\nLike I was saying, just looking at the razor on the counter makes me shake now. So the idea of holding that same blade up to my own face or neck... yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be terrified. Just the thought of it makes my stomach drop.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using the straight razor decrease if you were being supervised or assisted by someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't tried having someone stand by or help me with it since the fear got this bad. But... I mean, I doubt it would help much. \n\nIt's the razor itself that causes the panic. Having a friend or my boss right there wouldn't change the fact that I still have to hold the blade. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be just as anxious, even if someone I trusted was watching or trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about using the straight razor would be less intense if you were alone with the razor, but not at work?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. Like I said, just seeing the blade sets me off. It wouldn't really matter if I was at home, in my car, or at the shop. The razor is the problem, not where I am when I'm looking at it. I think I'd still have the exact same reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to see someone else holding or using it, even if you weren't the one using it?\n\nPatient: I haven't really stuck around to watch the other guys at the shop use one since this all got so bad, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... I mean, given how much just seeing a razor sitting on the counter bothers me, I imagine watching someone else hold it or use it would still make me really anxious. \n\nI don't know if it would be the exact same level of panic as having it in my own hand, but... yeah, I definitely wouldn't be comfortable anywhere near it, even if someone else was doing the shaving.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor decrease if you were to use it for a task that is not related to your work as a barber?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I haven't tried using it for anything else, but... the fear is really about the razor itself, not what I'm doing with it. \n\nEven if I was just using it to, I don't know, cut a piece of string or something at home, I'm pretty sure I'd still have the exact same panic. Just holding the blade, or even looking at it, is what causes the problem. So no, I don't think the task would make a difference.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be present if you were to encounter it in a completely unrelated context, such as a museum or an antique shop?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually run into one in a place like that, so I don't know for a fact. But... honestly, I'm pretty sure the anxiety would still be there. \n\nLike I was saying before, just the sight of the blade is what gets to me. So if I was walking through an antique store and just suddenly saw one sitting there on a shelf... yeah, I think my heart would still start racing. The context doesn't really seem to matter, it's just the razor itself.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you saw a straight razor being used for shaving in a movie or on television?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't actually seen one on TV or in a movie lately... at least, not since this whole thing got so bad. \n\nSo I don't know for a fact how I'd react to just seeing it on a screen. I might be okay since it's not real life, or it might still bother me, but I just haven't had that happen recently so I can't really say.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you saw a picture of a straight razor?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that either. I haven't exactly gone looking for pictures of them... I mean, I try to avoid them in general now. So, I don't know if a photo would give me that same panic or if I'd be fine since it's just a picture. I just haven't come across one to know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you saw a picture of a straight razor?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that, actually. But yeah, like I was saying, I really don't know. I haven't looked at any photos of them. I just try to avoid them completely, so I can't say for sure if a picture would make me panic or not.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you heard someone talk about using a straight razor?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think just hearing someone talk about it would bother me. It's more about actually being around one or having to handle it myself. Just a conversation wouldn't make me anxious.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you simply thought about a straight razor without seeing it or being near one?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just thinking about it doesn't bother me at all. It's the actual physical object that's the problem. I can think about them just fine without getting anxious, as long as I'm not actually around one.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be less intense if you were to see it in a context where it was being used for a purpose completely unrelated to shaving, such as a surgical procedure?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I've never been in a situation like that, so I can't say for certain. But just thinking about it... I feel like if the blade is there and it's exposed, it wouldn't matter what it's being used for. It's the object itself that causes the panic, you know? So I'd probably still be just as anxious.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor decrease if it were enclosed in a protective case?\n\nPatient: If it's fully enclosed in a case... yeah, I think that would help a lot. As long as I can't see the blade and I know it's put away where it can't cut anything, the anxiety goes way down. It's really the exposed blade that gets to me. So, if it's shut in a box or a thick leather pouch, I can handle being in the same room with it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I know it’s just a tool that I trained with, and I can use clippers all day long without a second thought. But... when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, it really feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or even cut myself really badly.\n\nDoctor: Is it the razor itself that scares you, or is it more the thought of what could happen if you were to make a mistake with it?\n\nPatient: I guess it's a little bit of both. I mean, the thought of making a mistake and really hurting someone is definitely what's in my head. But at this point... honestly, just looking at the razor itself makes me panic. Just seeing the blade sitting there on the counter is enough to make me start shaking, even before I pick it up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious if you were to handle the straight razor in a non-work setting, like at home?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I don't keep one at home, so I haven't really tested it out. But given how I react just seeing it on my station at work, I'd probably still feel pretty anxious if I had to hold one anywhere else. The blade itself just really gets to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel less anxious about using the straight razor if you were alone in the shop, with no clients present?\n\nPatient: I don't think it would make much of a difference, honestly. Even if the shop was completely empty... just the thought of holding it makes my chest tight. It's not just about the clients anymore. Like I said, just seeing the blade sitting there sets me off, so being alone probably wouldn't stop the shaking or the panic.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you had to use a straight razor for a purpose other than shaving a client, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually tried to shave myself with one since all this started, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... honestly, I think it would be just as bad. \n\nLike I was saying, just looking at the razor on the counter makes me shake now. So the idea of holding that same blade up to my own face or neck... yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be terrified. Just the thought of it makes my stomach drop.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using the straight razor decrease if you were being supervised or assisted by someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't tried having someone stand by or help me with it since the fear got this bad. But... I mean, I doubt it would help much. \n\nIt's the razor itself that causes the panic. Having a friend or my boss right there wouldn't change the fact that I still have to hold the blade. So, yeah, I'm pretty sure I'd still be just as anxious, even if someone I trusted was watching or trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about using the straight razor would be less intense if you were alone with the razor, but not at work?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. Like I said, just seeing the blade sets me off. It wouldn't really matter if I was at home, in my car, or at the shop. The razor is the problem, not where I am when I'm looking at it. I think I'd still have the exact same reaction.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to see someone else holding or using it, even if you weren't the one using it?\n\nPatient: I haven't really stuck around to watch the other guys at the shop use one since this all got so bad, so I don't know for absolute sure. But... I mean, given how much just seeing a razor sitting on the counter bothers me, I imagine watching someone else hold it or use it would still make me really anxious. \n\nI don't know if it would be the exact same level of panic as having it in my own hand, but... yeah, I definitely wouldn't be comfortable anywhere near it, even if someone else was doing the shaving.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor decrease if you were to use it for a task that is not related to your work as a barber?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, I haven't tried using it for anything else, but... the fear is really about the razor itself, not what I'm doing with it. \n\nEven if I was just using it to, I don't know, cut a piece of string or something at home, I'm pretty sure I'd still have the exact same panic. Just holding the blade, or even looking at it, is what causes the problem. So no, I don't think the task would make a difference.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be present if you were to encounter it in a completely unrelated context, such as a museum or an antique shop?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually run into one in a place like that, so I don't know for a fact. But... honestly, I'm pretty sure the anxiety would still be there. \n\nLike I was saying before, just the sight of the blade is what gets to me. So if I was walking through an antique store and just suddenly saw one sitting there on a shelf... yeah, I think my heart would still start racing. The context doesn't really seem to matter, it's just the razor itself.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you saw a straight razor being used for shaving in a movie or on television?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't actually seen one on TV or in a movie lately... at least, not since this whole thing got so bad. \n\nSo I don't know for a fact how I'd react to just seeing it on a screen. I might be okay since it's not real life, or it might still bother me, but I just haven't had that happen recently so I can't really say.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you saw a picture of a straight razor?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that either. I haven't exactly gone looking for pictures of them... I mean, I try to avoid them in general now. So, I don't know if a photo would give me that same panic or if I'd be fine since it's just a picture. I just haven't come across one to know for sure.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you saw a picture of a straight razor?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that, actually. But yeah, like I was saying, I really don't know. I haven't looked at any photos of them. I just try to avoid them completely, so I can't say for sure if a picture would make me panic or not.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you heard someone talk about using a straight razor?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think just hearing someone talk about it would bother me. It's more about actually being around one or having to handle it myself. Just a conversation wouldn't make me anxious.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you simply thought about a straight razor without seeing it or being near one?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just thinking about it doesn't bother me at all. It's the actual physical object that's the problem. I can think about them just fine without getting anxious, as long as I'm not actually around one.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be less intense if you were to see it in a context where it was being used for a purpose completely unrelated to shaving, such as a surgical procedure?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, honestly. I've never been in a situation like that, so I can't say for certain. But just thinking about it... I feel like if the blade is there and it's exposed, it wouldn't matter what it's being used for. It's the object itself that causes the panic, you know? So I'd probably still be just as anxious.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor decrease if it were enclosed in a protective case?\n\nPatient: If it's fully enclosed in a case... yeah, I think that would help a lot. As long as I can't see the blade and I know it's put away where it can't cut anything, the anxiety goes way down. It's really the exposed blade that gets to me. So, if it's shut in a box or a thick leather pouch, I can handle being in the same room with it.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be less intense if you were to use it for shaving someone you know personally, such as a family member, outside of a professional setting?\n\nPatient: I... no, I really don't think so. Even if it was my dad or my brother, you know, just doing it at home instead of at the shop... if that blade is open, I can't do it. It doesn't really matter who it is or where we are. The panic is just as severe because I'm still dealing with that exposed razor.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
